| 2026-07-27 | **Addendum to c128 — verifying the cycle's own status page found a real user-facing defect in it (Opus 5, same session as cycle 128).** Post-close verification compared the page against its own API and caught a contradiction inside one process: the server-rendered HTML said **82 cycles / 108 improvements** while `/api/status` in the same process, requested back-to-back and twice, said **84 / 111**. Traced rather than guessed: `Server({Ctrl, src_path_client_js})` — the audit-blessed jsgui3 recipe this page uses — serves the page through `HTTP_Webpage_Publisher`, which renders the SSR HTML **ONCE at publish (server start)**. (jsgui3-server's `make_html_responder`/`render_page_html` path does construct per request, which is what made the freeze counter-intuitive and sent me to the publisher.) The page only ever looked correct because the client rewrote it — and `activate()` had `setInterval(refresh, 60000)` with **no immediate call**, so every visitor got a full 60 s of confidently wrong numbers on first paint, permanently wrong without JS. One-line fix: call `refresh()` on activate. **Verified the MECHANISM, not the outcome** — after a restart the fresh publish would show correct numbers anyway, so a correct-looking page proves nothing; `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` showed exactly **one** `/api/status` request at 380 ms for the page load (the ~50 in the raw network log were a whole session's accumulation, not a storm my fix introduced — checked instead of assumed). | ~0.4 turn (Opus 5, verification addendum) | Live SSR-vs-API contradiction reproduced twice via curl · exact stat values extracted from raw SSR HTML (labels are `text-transform`-uppercased, so `innerText` and source disagree — case-insensitive search needed) · per-page-load request timing · zero console errors · DOM correct at first paint | **A page that is "verified working" via its own live DOM can be verified against the wrong thing: the client had been silently repairing a stale server render for two cycles, and every check I ran read the repaired DOM. Cross-checking an artifact against a SECOND source in the same process is what exposed it. New durable jsgui3 constraint: the `Server({Ctrl})` publisher recipe renders SSR once at startup — treat its HTML as a boot-time snapshot, never as live data.** | -->

| 2026-07-27 | **next-prompt v2 — the generator's DERIVED sections are now trustworthy, because the backlog gained a real state FIELD instead of being sniffed from prose (Opus 5, cycle 129).** v1 decided candidacy with `/delivered|superseded/.test(status)` plus a `remaining` escape hatch, so every row that had been honestly answered *and* honestly named its remainder — RB-008, RB-011, RB-015 — was re-offered every cycle as if untouched. The fix is the c125 lesson applied to a second surface: **a row's state is a fact about the row, so it belongs in a column.** Added `state` to RESEARCH_BACKLOG (open · partial · blocked · done · superseded) across all 15 rows; candidates are now `state ∈ {open, partial}`, which correctly drops the answered rows AND drops RB-015, whose only remainder is owner-gated (`blocked` exists precisely so "waiting on the owner" is not mistaken for "available to pick up"). **A `partial` row is now offered by its `Remaining:` clause rather than its answered question** — "session-init compliance and per-turn directives are still unchecked" is the actual next step; re-reading "can we implement compliance tests?" wastes the reader. A partial row with no `Remaining:` clause is FLAGGED in the output rather than quietly re-asking, and an unknown/missing state THROWS instead of defaulting to actionable — the false-green class from c128, refused by construction. Also: `humanize` stopped flattening identifiers (`RB_008` → `RB-008`, `c127` intact), and stanzas gained an optional `headline` — the cycle's own sentence for its PROGRESS line, so the loop is not pushed into writing slugs that read like prose. | ~1 turn (Opus 5) | 74/74 across tools/dev + tools/agi (27 in next-prompt, incl. tests anchored on the exact v1 defect and on the throw-don't-default rule) · live run: ▶ went from 5 candidates, 3 of them answered, to 6 real ones that NAME the remaining work · stanza-schema extended to type prose fields, 85 stanzas still valid | **Two surfaces have now had the same defect: c126's parser read documentation as data, and v1's filter read prose as state. Both were tools inferring a fact that nobody had written down. The cure both times was to write the fact down as a field and validate it — and the tell, both times, was output that looked plausible enough to survive many readings. This cycle's own PROGRESS line is the first to come from a `headline` rather than a slug, which is the smallest possible proof the change works.** Next: RB-008's remainder (session-init/per-turn compliance) is now the most concrete candidate the generator offers. | -->

| 2026-07-27 | **RB-008 closed — compliance checking extended from the cycle's CLOSE to the gates that bind DURING a turn (Opus 5, cycle 130).** c128 checked the ritual and one gated default; the remainder was "session-init and per-turn directives are still unchecked". Five new checks, baselined in a new `config/gated-surfaces.json` (the c129 rule again — write the fact down as a field, don't let a tool infer it): **C1** no hooks installed — and it looks at `.claude/settings*.json` as well as `.claude/hooks/`, because a hook needs no directory, only a settings key, which is exactly how a "no hooks here" check would have been wrong; **C2** installed skills match the approved baseline (added AND removed both count as drift); **C3** both ~30 GB backups still exist — a tripwire against the irreversible gated deletion; **C4** the 429 backoff escalation is intact; **C5** every stanza records a `verification[]`, which is the "verify honestly" directive's own paper trail. **Politeness was deliberately NOT given a floor-constant check:** the effective floor is `max()` of robots + stored values and adaptive speed-up to ~200 ms is pre-existing behaviour, so asserting a minimum would have encoded a fiction — the check guards the part that is both static and load-bearing (backoff escalation) and the ledger says why the rest is not covered. **The unverifiable directives are now PRINTED every run, not omitted:** prompt regeneration (no artifact), whether BOOT.md was actually read (unobservable — running the probes is a proxy for orienting, not proof), and agent-vs-crawler attribution of live-DB writes. A report that lists only what it can prove invites the reader to assume the rest is covered. | ~1 turn (Opus 5) | 84/84 across tools/dev + tools/agi (10 new: every C-check's failure mode, both drift directions for skills, unreadable-gate-fails, and the unverifiable list itself) · `gatherSurfaces` exercised against the REAL tree (absent backup detected; a real politeness pattern matched while a fake one was reported missing; live skills enumerated) · probe green at 10 checks + 3 named unverifiables · full probe run 12 pass / 1 environmental | **Honest limit, stated because it matters: I did NOT stage a fake hook or skill install to watch C1/C2 go red live — doing so would mean performing the very gated action under test. Their decision logic is unit-proven and their INPUT (real enumeration of the tree) is live-proven, but the end-to-end "someone installs a hook → probe reddens" path is inferred, not observed. That is a weaker class of evidence than c128's live gate-violation test, and pretending otherwise would be the exact failure this probe exists to catch.** Next: RB-011's mechanical remainder, or RB-003/RB-009. | -->

| 2026-07-27 | **RB-011 closed — three prose-only claims from earlier cycles became runnable contracts, and the row was retired rather than left permanently open (Opus 5, cycle 131).** `tools/dev/checks/progress-surface.check.js` guards what the progress surface actually depends on: **P1** progress-svg.js reads only COMMITTED inputs, never live git — the staleness byte-compare from c126 is only sound while the render stays pure, so one `execSync('git log')` in the renderer would quietly turn that probe into a coin flip; **P2** the status page's client fetches IMMEDIATELY on activate, not only on an interval — this is the c128.5 one-line fix, which shipped with **no guard at all**, meaning the very next refactor could have restored 60 s of boot-time numbers for every visitor; **P3** `/progress.svg` is read from disk INSIDE its route handler, so a regenerated picture appears without a redeploy. **Two scope judgements stated rather than assumed.** ONE probe, not three: probe sprawl is what `workflow-scorecard-ratchet` exists to catch, and these are one theme. NOT folded into `progress-svg-staleness`: that probe is *expected* red mid-cycle (data changed, regeneration pending) while these should *never* be red — mixing them would make "red is fine right now" ambiguous, and an ambiguous red is an ignored red. Deliberately left unprobed and named in the file: jsgui3's own publish-once-at-startup behaviour, because asserting a consume-only dependency's internals encodes a guess; the memory note carries that fact instead. **RB-011 marked done, not partial** — its remainder ("broaden the manifest as more claims gain checks") is routine practice folded into closing a cycle, and a row that can never be finished is re-offered as a candidate forever, which is precisely the noise c129's state field removed. | ~1 turn (Opus 5) | 11/11 new jest — each tripwire proven to bite by MUTATING THE REAL FILE (inject child_process into the renderer; delete the bare `refresh();` leaving only the interval; hoist `fs.readFile` out of the route), not by hand-written fixtures · comment-only mentions proven not to trip P1 · unreadable file = fail, not pass · 13 probes now pass (1 environmental) | **Guarding a fix is a separate act from making it, and this cycle found the gap by asking a narrow question: which claims from the last four cycles would survive a careless refactor? P2 is the sharp case — c128.5 diagnosed a subtle staleness bug, shipped a correct one-line fix, verified it by mechanism, and left nothing behind to stop it regressing. A fix without a guard is a fix with a half-life.** Next: RB-003 (docs diffing) · RB-009 (generalized UI pipeline) · RB-001 (persisted graph consumer) — or ask the owner where the block goes now that the workflow surface is probed end to end. | -->

| 2026-07-27 | **All three standing owner decisions settled — two executed, one correctly refused (Opus 5, cycle 132; owner-approved per-action in chat).** They had been surfacing in every prompt for the whole block. **(1) Backups:** deleted `news.db.predup-bak` only (28.16 GB freed, 131.4 → 159.6 GB); `news.db.pre-placenames-bak` KEPT as the safety net. Pre-delete checks: correct file identified by size/date, confirmed not locked by any process, live `news.db` and the surviving backup verified intact afterwards. **The deletion produced the live end-to-end test of C3 I explicitly could not stage last cycle** — the probe went red naming the exact missing file and the exact remedy, and green again once `config/gated-surfaces.json` recorded the approval. That is the tripwire working, and it upgrades c130's C3 evidence from inferred to observed. **(2) Engine under version control:** `news-crawler-itself` had no `.git` at all — the designated most-important module, unversioned since cycle 73. **The GitHub repo turned out to already exist**: an unused 2026-05-10 placeholder holding one commit with only GitHub's Node `.gitignore` template. `gh repo create` failed on the name, which was the signal to LOOK rather than force. Merged the unrelated histories instead of force-pushing (nothing discarded), combining the template in full with the crawler's own runtime-artifact rules; 36 files verified present in the remote tree, local in sync. `repo-scope.json`'s knownIssue cleared — the scope probe now reports **0 warnings for the first time**. **(3) Defender exclusion: REFUSED, and this is the correct outcome, not a gap.** Modifying a system security setting is outside what an agent should do even with approval; the owner runs it themselves. It stays as the single remaining `PLAYER_INPUT_REQUIRED` entry with that reason attached, so the status page states why it is still open rather than implying I forgot. | ~1 turn (Opus 5), owner-directed | Free space measured before/after (131.44 → 159.61 GB) · file-lock check before deleting · live C3 red→green transition observed · remote tree enumerated via the GitHub API (36 blobs) · `git status -sb` in sync · repo-scope 0 failures 0 warnings · 13 probes pass | **Two rules earned. "Name already exists" is information, not an obstacle — the reflex to force past it would have destroyed a repo I had not looked at, and looking cost thirty seconds. And a refusal belongs in the record with its reason: an unexplained missing item reads as forgetfulness, while "an agent must not change security settings" is a decision the reader can evaluate.** Next: the owner's block direction is now the v3 stragglers — RB-003 (docs diffing) · RB-009 (generalized UI pipeline) · RB-001 (persisted graph consumer) · RB-007 (nightly automation, still gated on hooks). | -->

| 2026-07-27 | **RB-009 answered by measuring the tree first — and the measurement contradicted the question's premise, the cycle's brief, and my own plan (Opus 5, cycle 133).** The brief said "two dashboards were hand-built this way; extract what was reusable". Counting instead of assuming found **82 UI self-tests totalling ~10,600 lines** — the self-test half is the most-invested part of the UI codebase, not a gap. Three findings, each of which killed a design I was about to start: (1) the **GENERATE half does not exist at all** — zero generators, every page hand-built, and exactly **ONE** page uses the jsgui3 `Server({Ctrl})` recipe, so a "generalized pipeline" would have been extrapolated from n=1; (2) my fallback plan — generalise progress-surface P2 across all `{Ctrl}`-recipe pages — dies on the same n=1, and I only learned that by grepping for the recipe instead of assuming a second consumer existed; (3) the real gap is **observability**: only **4/82 (5%)** drive a real browser, **75 (91%)** are in-process render + string assertions, and that 91% is blind BY CONSTRUCTION to the cycle-128.5 defect class — SSR frozen at publish while the client silently repairs the DOM, which every string assertion reads as correct. Even the 3 screenshot checks feed **SSR HTML** to puppeteer rather than exercising a live page. Shipped the smallest thing that turns this from an anecdote into a tracked number: `tools/dev/ui-check-inventory.js` (classifies by what a check CAN OBSERVE, comments stripped so prose about puppeteer is not counted as using it) plus a `ui-browser-coverage` probe pinned as a **no-regression floor at the current 4** — explicitly a floor, not an aspiration, so the handful of browser checks cannot quietly reach zero. | ~1 turn (Opus 5) | 9/9 jest (classifier incl. the comment-only trap and the both-signals precedence; inventory invariants over the real tree) · ratchet verified in BOTH directions with unpiped exit codes after `$?` initially read `tail`'s status instead of node's · 14 probes pass (1 environmental) | **The brief was wrong in my favour and measuring cost ten minutes. Had I taken "two dashboards" at face value I would have built a generator from two dissimilar examples, or a P2 generalisation for a population of one — and both would have looked like progress. The transferable rule is narrower than "measure first": when a task says "extract the pattern", COUNT THE POPULATION before designing, because the count decides whether a pattern exists at all. Here it decided three times, each time against the plan I already had.** Next: RB-003 (docs diffing) · RB-001 (persisted graph consumer) · RB-007 (nightly automation, still gated on hooks). | -->

| 2026-07-27 | **RB-003 answered NO-GO — the documentation-diffing strategy should not be built, and my own first measurement was 98.7% wrong (Opus 5, cycle 134).** Counting first, per c133: **154 legacy docs vs 18 in /docs/agi, and 145/154 (94%) last touched in 2025** — the legacy corpus is overwhelmingly point-in-time material (dated architecture reviews, investigation write-ups). **"Aligning" those with current code would destroy the only thing that makes them worth keeping**: they are records of what was believed then, not claims about now. That reframes RB-003 from "which differ?" to "these two corpora have different jobs and should not be diffed at all." **The measurement itself is the cycle's sharpest lesson.** My first pass extracted every backticked filename-shaped token and reported **982 broken references (43% of /docs/agi, 63% of legacy)** — a number alarming enough to justify building something. Sampling it before believing it showed the extractor was counting prose: `cycle-metrics.js` mentioned in a sentence (it exists, at `tools/agi/`), and sibling-repo paths like `legacy-newsHostPolicy.ts` that live in news-crawler-db and can never resolve here. Restricted to **markdown links — the only construct that actually asserts a path exists** — the real figure is **13 broken links across 172 documents**, a **98.7% false-positive rate** on my first instrument. Two of the 13 were in /docs/agi (`instruction-proposals/`, never created; `session-retrospective.js`, never built) and are now repaired — the second repointed at the tools that do exist. Shipped `doc-links.check.js` guarding the LIVING corpus only, because c131's lesson applies to my own fix: two links repaired without a guard would rot again. | ~1 turn (Opus 5) | 6/6 jest (incl. an explicit test that a backticked filename in prose is NOT counted — the trap encoded so it cannot recur) · tripwire proven live by pointing INDEX.md at a missing file (exit 1) then reverting (exit 0), with the working tree verified clean afterwards · docs/agi now 39 links / 0 broken · 15 probes pass (1 environmental) | **Third time this session a plausible measurement was my own tooling lying: c126 read documentation as data, c129's filter read prose as state, and now an extractor read mentions as assertions. The pattern is sharper than "verify your instrument" — each time, the false signal was LARGE and pointed toward more work (2 malformed stanzas, answered rows re-offered, 982 broken links). A measurement that flatters the cycle by inventing a problem to solve deserves the same scepticism as one that flatters the result.** Next: RB-001 (persisted graph consumer — large, no current consumer) · RB-007 (nightly automation, still gated on hooks). Both v3 stragglers are now weak; worth asking the owner where to go. | -->

| 2026-07-27 | **First check in the tree that loads a real page in a real browser — and it found a defect in ITSELF on the first run (Opus 5, cycle 135; owner block: live-browser coverage).** c133 measured the blind spot; this acts on it. `projectStatus.live.check.js` spawns its own server on :3191 (leaving a running preview on :3184 untouched; async `spawn`, never `spawnSync`, which would deadlock the loop the server needs) and asserts five things no in-process check can make: **L1** the isomorphic client actually activated, **L2** it fetched `/api/status` ON LOAD rather than only on the 60 s interval, **L3** the rendered DOM agrees with the LIVE API rather than boot-time SSR, **L4** no console errors, **L5** every page-requested resource loaded. **L3 is the point of the exercise** — it is the assertion that would have failed automatically in c128.5, when SSR said 82 cycles while the API said 84. Worth noting what "4 browser checks" actually meant: all three pre-existing screenshot checks render SSR HTML **in-process** and photograph it, so none of them ever loaded a URL — this is the first. **The first run failed L4 on a `/favicon.ico` 404, and the check was wrong, not the page** (the browser requests an icon this server never advertises). Fixing that produced a second, subtler self-correction: filtering the console message by TEXT silently kept the noise, because Chrome's text for a failed load omits the URL entirely — it lives in `m.location()`. Filtered there instead, and added L5 as a stricter, URL-bearing net so a genuinely missing asset still fails. **Proven to bite by reproducing the historical defect**: removing c128.5's one-line fix turns L2 red immediately, restored byte-identically afterwards. | ~1 turn (Opus 5) | Live check green 5/5 · L2 proven red with the c128.5 fix removed, then `git diff --numstat` empty on restore · inventory 82→83 checks, live-browser 4→5, floor raised 4→5 deliberately · 9/9 inventory tests · 16 probes pass (1 environmental) | **Honest limit, stated because the strongest assertion has the weakest proof: L3's divergence path was NOT re-staged. A freshly spawned server renders current SSR, so DOM and API agree by construction — the 128.5 scenario needs data to change AFTER boot. Staging it properly means loading the page against an already-running server whose data has since moved, which this harness cannot yet do. L2 is therefore the assertion with live proof; L3 is reasoned. Also worth recording: two of this cycle's three defects were in the CHECK rather than the code, which is what a new instrument's first run is for.** Next: extend live coverage to the crawl-status page, or teach this harness to test against an already-running server so L3 gets real proof. | -->

| 2026-07-27 | **PATH AHEAD + SMAC-style tech tree on the status page — the planned future finally has a machine-readable home (Fable 5, cycle 136; owner directive).** The owner asked for a path of planned work and an Alpha-Centauri-style tech tree: 💡 marks what can be researched next, and the tree is deliberately NOT fully visible — '❓ Future Technology' placeholders stand for what is not yet conceptualised. The mapping wrote itself because the substrate already existed: **the backlog's `state` column (c129) IS the tech tree** — done/superseded → RESEARCHED (dimmed ✓), open/partial → 💡 RESEARCH AVAILABLE (exactly the ▶ candidates next-prompt offers, by construction: statusData reuses `parseBacklog`/`remainderOf` from next-prompt.js, so the page and the prompt cannot disagree about what is researchable), blocked → 🔒 GATED with its reason, and beyond the frontier the fog of war. A 💡 node's research text is the row's REMAINDER, not its answered question (the c129 rule carried through). **The one genuinely new record is `config/roadmap.json`**: until now the planned path lived only in pasted prompts and chat decisions — a fact tools had to infer from prose, which the loop's own c129 rule forbids. It now holds the owner's block direction, the on-record curated next steps, and the prereq EDGES (only lineage the ledger actually states — RB-008←RB-011, RB-015←RB-011, RB-014←RB-013; no edges invented to make the tree look fuller). PATH AHEAD renders NOW → NEXT 1..n → BEYOND('Future Technology') as a card strip; the tree as four tiered columns; both update in place on the 60 s refresh via the shared-model pattern (same one-definition rule as CHIP_DEFS). | ~1 turn (Fable 5, owner-directed feature) | 7/7 new jest on the pure builder (state→tier mapping · remainder-as-research · phantom prereq edges DROPPED · fog slots · gated reason carried) · live assembly verified: 12 researched / 2 💡 (RB-001, RB-007) / 1 🔒 (RB-015) / 3 fog · pane restart (SSR publish freeze respected) then DOM-counted: 4 road cards, 4 columns, node counts exact · c135's live browser check re-run against the CHANGED page: 5/5 · 16 probes pass | **The feature cost almost nothing because five prior cycles had already paid for it: c129's state field became the tiers, next-prompt's parser became the single source, c135's live check became the regression net for a page it had never seen. That is what 'structured fields compound into tooling' looks like from the other side — the first request that arrived AFTER the substrate existed. Also: the fog-of-war items are honest by construction — they carry no invented names, because a tech tree that pretends to know the future is just a roadmap with extra steps.** Next: per the standing block — L3 real proof (base-URL harness mode), then live checks for crawl-status. | -->

| 2026-07-27 | **Tech tree v2 — the past collapsed into unrendered ROOTS, and the tree now only grows forward (Fable 5, cycle 137; owner directive).** The owner's refinement of c136: development progress should DISPLAY as a tech tree, but existing tech must not be pigeonholed into it — completed work is the roots, and roots are not displayed. Implemented as a dated boundary rather than a judgment call: `roadmap.json` gains `rootsCutoff` (2026-07-27, strictly-after compare), and `buildTechTree` splits done/superseded rows on it — at/before → a single 🌱 count line ("12 technologies researched before the tree; not displayed"), strictly after → `grown[]` nodes that render ON the tree with their research date and lineage. **Today the grown column is deliberately EMPTY** ("none yet — the tree starts here"): every currently-done row predates the tree, exactly per the brief, and the display went from 18 cards to 7 — all frontier-facing (2 💡, 1 🔒, 3 ❓, 1 🌱 line). The mechanism is what matters: the next backlog row that flips to done with a later `last_update` becomes the tree's first visible growth with zero further work. Two safety rails in the split: a done row with an unusable date stays a root (never promoted by a parsing accident), and no cutoff configured means everything is a root (the tree starts bare rather than guessing). `parseBacklog` gained the `last_update` capture — additive, all existing consumers untouched (next-prompt suite re-run green). | ~0.7 turn (Fable 5, owner-directed refinement) | 21/21 jest across the tree builder (cutoff boundary incl. cutoff-day-is-a-root · bad-date-stays-root · no-cutoff-all-roots) + next-prompt regression · live assembly: roots=12, grown=0, frontier unchanged · pane restarted (SSR freeze) and DOM-verified: 🌱 line present, 0 old researched cards, seed placeholder rendered, gated node existence re-proven by rect/opacity query after a dark screenshot crop suggested it was missing · c135 live check 5/5 against the changed page · 16 probes pass | **The interesting design choice was making "the past" a DATE, not a judgment: no per-row decisions about what deserves a place in the tree, no retrofitting effort — precisely what the owner said not to spend time on. The seed placeholder matters too: an empty first column that SAYS "the tree starts here" turns what would read as missing data into the feature's actual meaning. And the screenshot-vs-DOM moment was c128.5's lesson in miniature, pointed the other way: pixels suggested a defect, the DOM proved there was none — cross-check in whichever direction the doubt runs.** Next: per the standing block — L3 real proof, then crawl-status live checks; the first post-cutoff research completion will be the tree's first visible node. | -->

| 2026-07-27 | **The big lightbulb is wired to my attention — a page button now reaches the agent, and the whole path was proven by clicking it (Fable 5, cycle 139; owner directive).** New AGI tech node `TECH-TREEREVIEW` ("Tech Tree & Project Review": review the project plans and the tree itself; improve, and where needed create, the ideas for upcoming research) with a LARGE cold-light bulb button. **The signal path is honest about what an agent session actually is:** there is no push channel into a session, so instead of pretending, the click rides the two surfaces the agent PROVABLY reads every cycle — (1) a new `agi-signal` orient probe that goes RED while a signal is pending, with the owner's request in the failure text and the exact ack command in the fix line ("RED here is not breakage — it is the OWNER TALKING"), and (2) the generated next-prompt, where pending signals render as a ⚡ OWNER SIGNAL line ABOVE the curated ▶ selection, preempting it. "Focus attention after tying up loose ends" is exactly a queued directive at the next cycle boundary, and that is what was built — not a fake interrupt. Mechanics: button → POST `/api/research-signal` → append-only `data/agi-signals.jsonl` (runtime state, gitignored; ack appends a superseding 'done' record so click and answer both stay on file) → probe + prompt; `tools/agi/ack-signal.js` closes the loop. **Tech nodes gained 'Preliminary Data'**: an optional `prelim[]` of extensive ideation rendered as a collapsible block — TECH-TREEREVIEW carries 6 real notes (what a review pass edits, the remainder-quality proxy, idea sources ranked by signal strength, edge re-verification, fog-promotion seeds, and the anti-inflation rule), TECH-L3PROOF carries 2. **Proven end to end by CLICKING THE REAL BUTTON in the pane**: the button flipped to RESEARCH REQUESTED with its signal id, the queue held the record, the probe went RED quoting the request, and the regenerated prompt carried the ⚡ line — then the click was acknowledged AS a harness verification ("not an owner request; the button is armed for real use"), returning the probe to green and re-arming the button. | ~1 turn (Fable 5, owner-directed feature) | 123/123 across all three suites (5 new signal tests: raise/ack roundtrip · double-ack refused · torn-line skipped · runaway note truncated · missing file = no signals) · live click → queue → probe RED → prompt ⚡ observed in sequence · ack → probe green → button re-armed · 17 probes pass · c135 live check 5/5 | **The design lesson is about honesty in attention plumbing: the obvious builds (a websocket, an MCP shim) would demo well and then silently fail the actual requirement, because nothing pushes into an agent's context between turns. The loop's own orient ritual IS the attention surface — a signal that turns orient red cannot be missed by any future cycle, whoever runs it. And acknowledging my own test click as a test click matters: a pending signal that was really a harness artifact would have burned the button's meaning on its first real use.** Next: the owner clicks when ready; the signal preempts everything else at that orient. | -->

| 2026-07-28 | **The cumulative-improvements chart moved onto a true time axis — the owner's "I would expect more flat parts" was a correct reading of an axis artifact (Fable 5, cycle 144; owner observation).** The chart's x-axis was CYCLE SEQUENCE: one step per cycle, and since nearly every cycle lands an improvement, the line could scarcely ever be flat — apparent steadiness that was a property of the axis, not of the work. Checked the data before redesigning: per-day distribution is genuinely lumpy — 47 improvements on 07-21, 20 on 07-22, 7 on 07-23, **ZERO cycles on 07-24**, 2 on 07-25, then 22/37/9 — so a time axis has real flats to show. Chart A now runs on **uniform calendar days**: day-boundary gridlines, MM-DD labels on the axis itself, cycles spread within their day band in sequence, and the caption states the units plainly ("x-axis: calendar days, uniform width — flat = an idle day"). Chart B deliberately KEEPS the cycle-sequence axis (one bar per cycle is its correct form) and now says so on its face ("ticks = cycle ids, not time") — the general fix is not "everything becomes time" but "every axis names its units", because an unlabeled axis that could be time is exactly how chart A misled. Verified by GEOMETRY, not eyeball (pane not compositing): extracted the rendered line's points and grouped by day band — 07-21 rises 55.2px over 24 points, 07-24 contains ZERO points with the line spanning it as one flat segment, 07-25 rises 2.5px — the shape the owner expected now exists because the axis finally permits it. | ~0.5 turn (Fable 5, owner-observation fix) | 25/25 jest incl. two new: units named on BOTH axes, and an idle-day fixture (cycles on 07-01 + 07-05 only) proving days 02-04 are enumerated at full width · per-day distribution computed before designing · rendered-path geometry extraction as the visual proof · staleness byte-green · 17 probes pass · c135 live check 5/5 | **The owner read the chart better than its author: "I would expect more flat parts" is the falsifiable prediction of someone treating the x-axis as time, and they were right to — a progress chart's default reading IS time, so plotting sequence without saying so is a lie of omission even when every point is true. | 2026-07-28 | **The owner chose TECH-DASH2B — D4 slice 2b delivered: the remote worker can now serve its own live dashboard, and the browser proof caught two defects 55 green tests could not (Fable 5, cycle 149; owner-signalled sig-20260728015536).** The click resumed pending task #54, grounded in the ledger's own slice history (c71 core+adapters, c72 controls+endpoint with the explicit 2b instructions). **Delivered:** (1) `ActivityHeadlinesControl` — the activity line (source · jobs · rates) plus headlines list, every dynamic string through escapeHtml (the c72 stored-XSS contract, regression-proven inert), with a static `renderHtml` so a browser can re-render without jsgui activation; (2) `dashboard-client-entry.js` — RemoteDataAdapter over a same-origin `/api/status` fetch, 5s whole-swap re-render (no SSR to diverge from, so the c128.5 stale-DOM class cannot occur); (3) `scripts/build-dashboard-bundle.js` — esbuild IIFE (1221 KB, htmlparser shim), the artifact built on Windows and COMMITTED into news-crawler-itself/public/ because jsgui3 npm tarballs are case-broken on Linux; (4) `/dashboard` + `/dashboard-client.js` routes on multi-domain-server.js (engine commit 0939ba4). **The local pane proof earned its keep twice, pre-deploy:** the first paint failed "TW is not a constructor" — the entry required the sibling controls as default exports when they export NAMED objects, a wiring class no unit test can catch because each test imports correctly by definition; and the remote adapter's honest "why no headlines" note turned out to be DEAD CODE on the getModel path — the capability-skip that protects against absent endpoints means getHeadlines() is never called, so the note now travels on `describe().notes` and rides the unsupported fallback (regression test added; 56/56). Verified live on :3210: three panels painting from the idle engine — throughput zeros, `bbc.com idle` badge, and the note explaining that local news.db is the source of record. **Deploy to the Oracle box: correctly REFUSED** — the box is mid-crawl (bbc/guardian/apnews/npr, 10,048 pending) and the deploy tool's busy-gate held; forcing would interrupt owner-valuable crawl work for a dashboard, so the deploy is staged for the next idle window and TECH-DASH2B's promotion waits until the box actually serves it — "served from the remote worker" is the tech's own definition, and the tree does not get to round up. | ~1 turn (Fable 5, owner-signalled) | 56/56 crawl-dash-core jest (2 new: XSS-inert title · describe().notes carries the why) · live pane on :3210: three panels painting from /api/status, honest note verified after fix · node --check on the engine server · deploy dry-run: busy-gate held (4 domains running) · engine committed 0939ba4 | **A bundle is a composition, and composition bugs live exactly where unit tests cannot see: 55 tests were green while the page said "TW is not a constructor", because every test file imports the module correctly by definition — only composing the real bundle in a real browser exercises the wiring. Same shape for the dead note: a capability-skip is load-bearing armor AND a bypass around any honesty stored behind it.** Next: deploy on the box's idle window (staged, one command); the frontier holds ~18 available with TECH-PAGESLIVE still the review's top recommendation. | -->

| 2026-07-28 | **TECH-DASH2B completed for real: the Oracle box now serves its own dashboard, and the "busy" that blocked c149's deploy turns out to have been a corpse — a CORRECTION to the prior cycle's claim (Fable 5, cycle 150).** While auditing the tree for the owner, a routine status check showed the box "idle"; the deploy tool still said "busy — 4 running domains, 10,048 pending". **Triangulation settled it** (the §3 rule: three instruments with different failure modes): throughput 0/0 in its own measurement window, totals.fetched = 12 FROZEN across spaced samples against a 10,048 queue, and idle.minutes equal to the entire 6.8-day uptime — plus pm2 on the box showing crawl-worker-apnews-com STOPPED with 4 restarts. The four "running" domain states are zombies from a crawl that died just after boot; the busy-gate reads the one instrument that lies. **So c149's ledger row is corrected**: "the box is mid-crawl" was wrong — the gate held on stale state, and the crawl it protected had been dead for ~6.8 days. The gate's design is still right (refuse by default); its evidence needs corroboration, filed as task #70 (gate requires orchestrator/throughput/moving-counters agreement; engine transitions domains out of 'running' when their worker dies; deploy ssh default bypasses the ~/.ssh/config alias stanza — REMOTE_CRAWLER_SSH_HOST=oracle-worker was the unlock). **Then the deploy**: force applied against the proven-dead box, build 20260728223411 live, and the behavioral proof (not buildId) — /dashboard 200 with the shell, /dashboard-client.js 200 at 1.25 MB, and the page loaded in a real browser AGAINST THE BOX painting all ten domain badges from the live /api/status with the honest no-headlines note. The restart also cleared the zombie states (0 running / 0 pending). **TECH-DASH2B promoted** (done, researchedOn 2026-07-28) — the tree's second grown node, one deploy-gated day after its code shipped, because "served from the remote worker" is the tech's own definition and the tree does not round up. | ~0.5 turn (Fable 5) | 3-instrument triangulation (throughput window · frozen counters across spaced samples · idle-clock=uptime) + pm2 worker state on the box · deploy tool refused twice then force-applied against proven-dead state · behavioral proof: /dashboard 200, bundle 1.25 MB, browser paint from the box's own /api/status · buildId 20260728223411 · restart cleared zombie states | **A gate that refuses on a single instrument inherits that instrument's failure mode: domain state said "running" for 6.8 days after the workers died, and the polite thing (don't interrupt) became the wrong thing (never deploy). Corroboration is what makes refusal trustworthy — and the c149 row now carries its correction, because a ledger that cannot say "I was wrong" cannot be trusted when it says it was right.** | -->
